## Platform, security, handoffs, build/deploy, tests & docs

### Feedback API: functions/api/feedback.ts, functions/utils/rateLimit.ts, shared/feedbackConstants.ts
- [ ] [SEC·H·M] `functions/api/feedback.ts:221-244,287` — Only the `Origin` check guards issue creation, and non-browser clients can fake that header. Turnstile is enforced only when `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` is set, and STATUS.md:93 says no keys are set (the built `index.html` has `turnstileSiteKey:""`). The limiter is an in-isolate `Map` (`rateLimit.ts:8`). So `curl -H 'Origin: https://neospace.ibm.io'` can file unlimited public issues using the repo PAT. → Set up Turnstile in prod, and add a Cloudflare WAF rate-limit rule (or KV/DO counter) on `/api/feedback`. Consider refusing in production when no Turnstile secret exists. · decision
  - *Verifier:* The Origin check is spoofable and Turnstile is off: `docs/STATUS.md:93` says "keys not set", and the built `index.html` has `turnstileSiteKey:""`. But "unlimited" overstates it. `allowRequest` still caps at 5 per hour per IP per isolate, so spam scales with IPs and isolates rather than being unlimited from one client. Impact still High-ish (public issue spam via the PAT).
- [ ] [UX·H·M] `functions/api/feedback.ts:335-348` — NOT FIXED (audit #873, "upload to R2"). The screenshot is still inlined as `![screenshot](data:image/…;base64,…)`. GitHub's sanitizer drops `data:` image sources, so the issue shows no image, only up to 45 KB of base64 bloat. The UI tells users "any screenshot is included". → Upload to R2 (or the GitHub contents API) and link the URL, or remove the feature and its copy. · decision ✓
- [ ] [UX·M·S] `functions/api/feedback.ts:305-313` — `escapeMd` rewrites `@` to `&#64;` and `#N` to `&#35;N`. The body then goes inside a ```` ```text ```` fence, where GitHub does not decode entities. Every Fediverse handle in a note shows as `&#64;alice&#64;server`. The fence alone already stops mentions and cross-refs. → Don't run `escapeMd` on the fenced body (keep it only for text outside a fence). · mech
- [ ] [SEC·M·S] `functions/api/feedback.ts:225-237` — The rate limit is charged before validation and before Turnstile. Junk JSON or failed verifications use up the shared 5/hour bucket, so one abuser behind the same NAT or IPv6 /64 can lock out real users for an hour. → Increment the bucket only after Turnstile passes, or keep a separate failure bucket. · judgment
- [ ] [TOOLING·M·S] `functions/api/feedback.ts:35-62` — Two overlapping hand-kept allowlists, with `pages.dev` special-cased in both. `localhost:3000` is allowed, but `npm run cf:preview` (wrangler on :8788), `.claude/launch.json`'s pages dev on :3013 and nuxt dev on :3011 are not, so feedback returns 403 `FORBIDDEN_ORIGIN` in the documented preview flow. → Use a same-origin check (`Origin === new URL(request.url).origin`) plus the prod host. · judgment
- [ ] [CODE·L·S] `functions/api/feedback.ts:327` — The meta block hardcodes `source: neospace.ibm.io leave-a-note`, even for pages.dev previews and localhost, so triage can't tell preview reports from prod ones. → Build it from `new URL(request.url).host`. · mech
- [ ] [SEC·L·S] `functions/api/feedback.ts:64-77` — Cloudflare Pages does not apply `_headers` to Function responses, so `/api/feedback` replies have no nosniff, HSTS or frame headers. → Add `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` (and friends) in `json()`. · mech
- [ ] [UX·L·S] `functions/api/feedback.ts:303` + `app/components/FeedbackNotes.vue:173` — The client prepends `[ux] ` to a title that can already be 200 characters (`maxlength=FEEDBACK_TITLE_MAX`). The server then cuts to 200, silently losing the end of a max-length title. The prefix also uses the lowercase id (`[ux]`) where the UI says "UX", and repeats the label the server already adds. → Drop the client prefix (labels carry the kind), or make the input's maxlength account for it. · mech
- [ ] [CODE·L·S] `functions/api/feedback.ts:24-27` + `functions/tsconfig.json:10` — Hand-rolled `PagesFunction` type (no `waitUntil` or `params`), and `noImplicitAny: false` weakens `strict`. → Use `@cloudflare/workers-types` and remove the override. · mech
- [ ] [CODE·L·S] `shared/feedbackConstants.ts:25-30` — `FEEDBACK_KIND_LABELS` maps every kind to itself, which is pointless indirection. → Use `kind` directly. · mech

### Feedback client: FeedbackNotes.vue, useFeedbackNotes.ts, app/utils/feedback.ts, app/utils/log.ts
- [ ] [SEC·H·S] `app/utils/feedback.ts:73-76` — Up to 40 log lines go into every public issue: home-server URLs (`notifications.ts:256` `${inst.url}`), instance ids and stack traces. The lede (`FeedbackNotes.vue:280-281`) mentions only "the current page URL and any screenshot". → Disclose this and add an "Include recent app logs (shows your server)" opt-in, off by default. Strip hostnames when it's off. · decision ✓
- [ ] [UX·M·S] `app/utils/feedback.ts:74-76` + `functions/api/feedback.ts:309-313` — The `<details><summary>Recent client logs` wrapper is sent inside the user body, which the server fences as `text`. It shows as literal HTML instead of a collapsible. The logs also count toward the 8,000-character `FEEDBACK_BODY_MAX`, so long notes cut the logs mid-line. → Send `logs` as its own field and have the server render it in its own `<details>` and fence. · judgment
- [ ] [UX·M·S] `app/components/FeedbackNotes.vue:190` — `getGitHubNewIssueUrl` runs for every failure, including 429, Turnstile and network errors where the link is never shown. For long notes it overwrites the user's clipboard without saying so (`app/utils/feedback.ts:50-58`). → Build the fallback URL only in the branches that show it. · mech
- [ ] [UX·M·S] `app/utils/feedback.ts:42-58` — The truncated body says "[Full text copied to clipboard — URL truncated]" before the copy is even tried. The copy runs after an awaited fetch, when Safari has usually dropped user activation, and failures are swallowed, so the note is often false. → Add the note only if `writeText` succeeds, or show a "Copy note" button. · mech
- [ ] [UX·M·S] `app/components/FeedbackNotes.vue:199-204` + `app/utils/feedback.ts:99-104` — Raw messages reach the toast. Safari's "Load failed" misses the network regex, Docker's `{"error":"not_available"}` shows as "not_available", and a non-JSON reply (nuxt dev 404) shows the first 120 characters of HTML. → Map by status and code, and never show server text. · mech
- [ ] [UX·M·S] `app/components/FeedbackNotes.vue:200` — It says "Couldn't reach GitHub", but it's the NeoSpace API that failed. The offline case also doesn't offer the prefilled GitHub link, which would work once the user is back online. → Change the copy to "Couldn't send — check your connection", set `fallbackUrl`, and keep the retry. · mech
- [ ] [UX·M·S] `app/utils/feedback.ts:78-91` + `app/components/FeedbackNotes.vue:88-90,272,386` — The fetch has no timeout, and Close, Cancel, Escape and the backdrop all do nothing while `busy`. The server can hold the request for 10 s (Turnstile) plus 30 s (GitHub), and a stalled connection leaves the user stuck in the modal. → Add `AbortSignal.timeout(~45s)` and let Cancel abort. · mech
- [ ] [UX·M·S] `app/components/FeedbackNotes.vue:186-204` — Turnstile tokens work only once. After a 502 or 429 the spent token stays in `turnstileToken`, so the user's retry fails with `TURNSTILE_FAILED`. → Call `resetTurnstile()` after every server response, not only on success or a Turnstile error. · mech
- [ ] [UX·M·S] `app/components/FeedbackNotes.vue:193-195` + `functions/api/feedback.ts:287-294` — If the secret is set but the build lacks `NUXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY`, there's no widget and every submit gets 403 `TURNSTILE_REQUIRED`. The user sees "Verification failed — try again." forever, with no GitHub fallback. → Without a site key, treat `TURNSTILE_REQUIRED` like `NOT_CONFIGURED` (show the fallback link). · judgment
- [ ] [A11Y·M·S] `app/components/FeedbackNotes.vue:210-213` — `initialFocus: 'input, textarea, .notes-panel__close'` takes the first match in document order: the visually hidden "UX" radio (`:292-297`), not Title. Keyboard and screen-reader users start on Note type. → `initialFocus: '#neospace-note-title'`. · mech
- [ ] [UX·M·M] `app/utils/feedback.ts:139-159` — `shrinkImageDataUrl` caps size at 1280 px and then only lowers JPEG quality down to 0.42. A tall phone screenshot (about 591×1280 after scaling) often stays over the 40,000-character budget, so the most common upload gets "Screenshot too large". → Shrink the dimensions in steps (1280→960→720→540) before giving up. · judgment
- [ ] [UX·L·S] `app/utils/feedback.ts:165-175` — Files under 40 KB skip re-encoding and any type check. A small SVG, HEIC or AVIF shows in the preview, then the server drops it ("not a PNG/JPEG/WebP/GIF"). → Always re-encode through canvas, or check `file.type` against the server's list. · mech
- [ ] [UX·L·S] `app/utils/feedback.ts:149-154` — A transparent PNG re-encoded as JPEG gets a black background (no fill before `drawImage`), so dark-on-transparent screenshots come out unreadable. → `ctx.fillStyle='#fff'; ctx.fillRect(...)` first. · mech
- [ ] [UX·L·S] `app/components/FeedbackNotes.vue:353-361` — "Remove" clears `shot` but not `fileRef.value`. Choosing the same file again fires no `change`, so it can't be re-attached. → Clear the input in the Remove handler (as `reset()` does). · mech
- [ ] [CODE·M·M] `app/composables/useFeedbackNotes.ts:50-55` — A raw `history.pushState({ neoFeedback: true }, '')` skips vue-router's state (`back`, `current`, `position`), and it's the only raw pushState in the app. After closing, browser Forward lands on an entry the router doesn't know (warning, broken scroll restoration), and back from it does nothing visible. → Merge `history.state` into the pushed state (`{ ...history.state, neoFeedback: true }`), or use a router hash/query. · judgment
- [ ] [DESIGN·L·M] `app/components/FeedbackNotes.vue:72-78,411-415,787-816` — It has its own toast (position, timing, styles) and a second live region instead of `useToastStore` (used in `AlgorithmSheet.vue:11`, `AccountSwitcher.vue:52`). Feedback toasts look and sit differently from every other toast. → Use the toast store. · judgment
- [ ] [UX·L·S] `app/components/FeedbackNotes.vue:280-281` — The lede promises "The current page URL", but only the path is sent (`app/utils/feedback.ts:86-88`, and the server strips the host). → "…the page path, recent app logs (if allowed), and any screenshot are included." · mech
- [ ] [CODE·M·M] `app/utils/log.ts:50-70` — 35 raw `console.*` calls skip the ring buffer (e.g. `notifications.ts:363,446,540`, `status.ts:281`, `useAccountSearch.ts:54`), so feedback logs miss most real failures. Those `console.warn` calls also print in prod, unlike `logWarn`. → Send them through `logWarn`/`logError`. · judgment
- [ ] [SEC·L·S] `app/utils/log.ts:32-35` — Redaction gaps. `SECRET_PARAM` needs a `?&#;` before the key, so a logged form body that starts `code=…` or `access_token=…` is kept. `SECRET_JSON_KEY` doesn't cover `"code"`, `"id_token"` or `"state"`. → Add `(^|[?&#;\s])` and those keys, plus tests. · mech
- [ ] [CODE·L·S] `app/utils/log.ts:72-78,88-90` — `logDebug`, `logInfo` and `getLogRing` have no callers outside tests; `app/utils/feedback.ts:12-16` re-exports `FEEDBACK_GITHUB_REPO`, which no one imports. → Remove the dead exports. · mech

### Suite handoffs: useLoomHandoff.ts, composeHandoff.ts, loom/bruh/notebookHandoff.ts
- [ ] [CODE·M·S] `app/composables/useLoomHandoff.ts:190-199` — The dispatcher checks origins in a fixed order. `isBruhOrigin` accepts any localhost (`bruhHandoff.ts:36`) and `isNotebookOrigin` accepts every `*.ibm.io` (`notebookHandoff.ts:37`). A `loom-neospace-share` from `https://loom.ibm.io` goes to `onNotebookMessage`, which rejects the type and returns, so it never reaches the Loom branch. Local Loom and Throughline messages hit the bruh branch the same way. → Dispatch on `e.data.type` first, then check that source's origin rule. · mech
  - *Verifier:* Mis-dispatch is real: `https://loom.ibm.io` passes `isNotebookOrigin` (`.ibm.io`), `onNotebookMessage` rejects the type, and the message never reaches the Loom branch; localhost is caught by `isBruhOrigin` first. In prod, though, this path is currently unreachable because COOP same-origin (#24) already severs the opener channel. It bites in dev, and in prod once #24 is fixed. Impact: Medium now, High once #24 is fixed.
- [ ] [CODE·H·M] `public/_headers:7` + `app/composables/useLoomHandoff.ts:108-123,233-244` — With `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin`, NeoSpace opened by a cross-origin Loom, bruh or Throughline tab gets `window.opener === null`. The ready pings, ACKs, Loom image buffers and bruh "full caption" upgrade never happen in prod; only `?compose=` works. → Choose: serve `/` with `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: unsafe-none` (the opened page's COOP decides), or remove the postMessage path, its 10-second ping loop and the docs promising it. · decision ✓
- [ ] [SEC·M·S] `app/utils/notebookHandoff.ts:36-37` — It trusts every `*.ibm.io` subdomain (loom, bruh, neospace itself, anything hosted later) to push compose text, which makes `NOTEBOOK_ORIGINS` (`:8-11`) pointless. → Use an exact allowlist (`ibm.io`, `www.ibm.io`, `notebook.ibm.io`). · mech
- [ ] [CODE·M·S] `app/composables/useLoomHandoff.ts:78-106` — ACKs go to `window.opener`, not `e.source`. If the share came from another window or frame, or the opener navigated away, the sender never gets an ACK and keeps retrying. The three ack functions are also copy-pasted. → One `ack(e, type)` that calls `(e.source as Window)?.postMessage(..., e.origin)`. · mech
- [ ] [UX·M·M] `app/stores/composeHandoff.ts:341-347,130` — postMessage images of 400 KB or more are never turned into a data URL. A signed-out user is sent to `/login` (`useLoomHandoff.ts:224-226`), the saved share has no image, and the chart is gone after OAuth. → Keep large buffers in IndexedDB or the Cache API, keyed by share. · judgment
- [ ] [CODE·L·S] `app/stores/composeHandoff.ts:49` vs `:48,341-342` — GIF and AVIF buffers pass `ALLOWED_IMAGE_TYPES` and get saved as data URLs, but `DATA_URL_IMAGE` accepts only png, jpeg and webp, so a saved GIF or AVIF share is dropped on reload or login. → Use the same type list in both. · mech
- [ ] [CODE·L·S] `app/stores/composeHandoff.ts:159-163,308-316` — `readPersistedLoomShare` and `ingestFromQuery` (which also leaves out `'notebook'`) have no callers. `openLoomCompose` (`useLoomHandoff.ts:326`) is unused, and the deprecated `CHART_ALT_MAX` alias is still in use (`loomHandoff.ts:48,100`). → Delete them, and use `MEDIA_ALT_MAX` directly. · mech
- [ ] [UX·L·S] `app/stores/composeHandoff.ts:167-171,298-301` — `loading`, `error` and `storyStates.phase` are never read (`RealComposeBox.vue:778-831` reads only `hasPending`/`take`), so a failed story fetch never reaches the user. The error copy also says "Failed to load Loom share" for bruh and Throughline, and the no-image notice (`:117`) is missing its full stop. → Show `error` in the compose notice, or drop the state, and fix the copy. · judgment
- [ ] [SEC·L·S] `app/composables/useLoomHandoff.ts:255-262` + `app/stores/composeHandoff.ts:110-114` — Any site can link `?compose=bruh&text=…` or `?compose=notebook&text=…`, and compose then shows the trusted labels "Page excerpt + link from bruh" or "Cell from Throughline" over text the attacker chose. → For query-sourced shares, use a neutral notice ("Prefilled from a link — review before posting"). Keep the source label for origin-checked messages. · judgment
- [ ] [CODE·L·S] `app/composables/useLoomHandoff.ts:279-280` — NOT FIXED (audit "make it event-driven", AUDIT:198). There's still a fixed 1.2 s sleep before checking a saved share, which slows every cold boot's handoff. → Resolve when a share message arrives or a short timeout fires, whichever is first. · judgment
- [ ] [CODE·L·S] `app/composables/useLoomHandoff.ts:111-119` — Ready pings go only to the fixed prod origin sets, while the `is*Origin` checks accept localhost and preview hosts. Preview and local siblings never receive "ready", so their handshake can't finish. → The ping carries no data, so post it with `'*'`, or target `new URL(document.referrer).origin` when it passes the origin check. · judgment
- [ ] [UX·L·S] `app/composables/useLoomHandoff.ts:265` — `router.replace({ …, query: {} })` drops every query param, not only `compose`/`story`/`text`. → Delete only the handoff keys. · mech
- [ ] [CODE·L·S] `app/stores/composeHandoff.ts:172` — In-flight Promises are kept in Pinia state (`_ingestPromises`), where they get proxied and serialized by devtools. → Use a module-level `Map`. · mech

### PWA, service worker & boot: nuxt.config.ts, public/boot/rescue.js, plugins
- [ ] [UX·M·M] `nuxt.config.ts:43,97-98` — REGRESSED (audit #913 says "already registerType:'prompt', skipWaiting:false"). It's back to `autoUpdate` with `skipWaiting` and `clientsClaim`. With no `onNeedReload`, vite-plugin-pwa's `register.js:39-46` calls `window.location.reload()` when the new SW activates. Updates are checked every 30 min plus on focus/visibility (`pwa-rescue.client.ts:23-38`), so tabs reload mid-use: attachments, open threads, column scroll, Edward state and the feedback draft are lost. → `registerType: 'prompt'` with an "Update ready" toast, or keep autoUpdate but delay the reload until the page is hidden or the next navigation. · decision
  - *Verifier:* The mechanics are right: autoUpdate plus skipWaiting/clientsClaim, no `onNeedReload` in `@vite-pwa/nuxt` (`pwa.client.js`), and `register.js:39-46` calls `window.location.reload()`, with update checks every 1800s plus on focus. But it isn't a regression: it's a deliberate change, commented at :41-42 as fixing stale home-screen shells. Corrected: "autoUpdate (chosen to fix stale PWA shells) reloads open tabs mid-use after each deploy; defer the reload until hidden or next navigation." Impact: Medium/High depending on deploy frequency.
- [ ] [PERF·M·S] `nuxt.config.ts:94` — `globPatterns` precaches every JS chunk and font, about 2.6 MB. That includes the three.js chunk (`_nuxt/4gI5Z-_B.js`, 737 KB) and both OpenDyslexic woff2 files (235 KB), downloaded on first visit by everyone and again after deploys. → Leave the Edward/three and font files out of the precache, and add CacheFirst `runtimeCaching` for `/_nuxt/` and `/fonts/`. · judgment
- [ ] [PERF·L·S] `nuxt.config.ts:94-95` — The generated `sw.js` lists `manifest.webmanifest` twice and precaches 7 prerendered shells (`profile`, `messages`, `login`, `groups`, `explore`, `notifications`, `algorithms/import`) that are byte-identical to `/`. → globIgnores `**/*/index.html` (`navigateFallback: '/'` already covers them). · mech
- [ ] [CODE·M·S] `app/plugins/mount-signal.client.ts:2-10` — It signals "mounted" while plugins initialize, before `app.mount` and layout setup. A crash in layout or page setup leaves a blank screen with the `rescue.js:60-69` watchdog already turned off. → Signal from `nuxtApp.hook('app:mounted')` (or `page:finish`). · mech
- [ ] [CODE·L·S] `public/boot/rescue.js:45-48` — The window `error` listener isn't registered in the capture phase, so it never sees `<script type=module>` load failures (resource errors don't bubble). A 404 on the entry chunk leaves a blank page for the full 20–45 s watchdog. → `addEventListener('error', fn, true)` and check `e.target.src` for `/_nuxt/`. · mech
- [ ] [UX·M·M] `nuxt.config.ts:49-89` — The manifest has no `share_target`, though the app already accepts `?compose=…&text=` and a social client is an obvious share target. It also has no `shortcuts` (Compose, Notifications, Messages) and no `screenshots` (needed for Chrome's richer install sheet). · decision
- [ ] [DESIGN·L·S] `nuxt.config.ts:53-54` vs `:125` — Manifest `theme_color` and `background_color` are fixed to dark `#1a1a18`, while light mode uses `#faf9f7`. Light-theme users get a dark splash and a dark title bar in the installed app. · decision
- [ ] [UX·M·S] `nuxt.config.ts:116-139` — There are no Open Graph or Twitter meta tags (`og:title`, `og:description`, `og:image`). Links to neospace.ibm.io shared on Mastodon, the app's own audience, unfurl as bare URLs. → Add OG tags and a 1200×630 image under `public/`. · mech
- [ ] [DESIGN·L·S] `public/icons/icon.svg:3-14` + `nuxt.config.ts:47` — `icon.svg` is precached but never used (no `rel=icon` SVG link, not in the manifest). It draws "NS" with `<text>` in IBM Plex Sans, a font SVG-as-image can't load, so it renders in a fallback face. → Convert the text to outlines and add it as `sizes:"any"`, or delete it. · mech
- [ ] [CODE·L·S] `nuxt.config.ts:44-48` — `includeAssets` repeats files `globPatterns` already covers (`ico`, `png`, `svg`). → Remove it. · mech
- [ ] [CODE·L·S] `nuxt.config.ts:102` + `app/plugins/pwa-rescue.client.ts:20-38` — Two update pollers: `periodicSyncForUpdates: 1800` and the plugin's focus/visibility `reg.update()`. Together with D1, just focusing the tab can trigger a reload. → Keep one. · judgment
- [ ] [PERF·L·S] `nuxt.config.ts:35` — NOT FIXED (audit #931, "bundle budget"). `chunkSizeWarningLimit: 120` is only a warning, and the 737 KB three chunk sets it off on every build, so the warning is noise and no budget is enforced. → Add a `size-limit`-style CI check on the entry chunks, and raise the warning limit for known lazy chunks. · judgment
- [ ] [DESIGN·L·S] `nuxt.config.ts:52` vs `:117` — Two different descriptions ("A multi-column Fediverse client — feeds, groups, and multi-server logins." vs "NeoSpace — a multi-column Fediverse client."). → Use one string. · mech

### Hosting, headers & Docker: _headers, _redirects, spa-fallback.mjs, nginx, Dockerfile
- [ ] [UX·M·S] `scripts/spa-fallback.mjs:23-24` — NOT FIXED (audit #920, "top-level 404.html disables SPA mode… drop both"). Copying `index.html` to `404.html` turns off Cloudflare Pages' SPA routing, so deep links (`/status/123`, `/groups/cats`) come back as HTTP 404 with the app shell. Link unfurlers, uptime checks and crawlers treat them as dead. → Delete `404.html` (and Nuxt's `200.html`) in the post-step so Pages answers 200, then verify with `curl -I`. · mech
- [ ] [SEC·M·S] `docker/nginx-default.conf:18` — The Docker CSP keeps `'unsafe-inline'` with no hashes (`spa-fallback.mjs` rewrites only `_headers`), so injected inline script runs. It also has no HSTS or `upgrade-insecure-requests`, though `:12` says it mirrors `_headers`. → Generate the nginx `add_header` lines from `.output/public/_headers` in the Dockerfile build stage. · judgment
- [ ] [SEC·M·S] `Dockerfile:3,13` — NOT FIXED (audit #924, "pinned by digest"). Neither stage is pinned by digest, and `nginx-unprivileged:1.27-alpine` is an ended mainline branch (current: 1.28 stable, 1.29 mainline). → `1.28-alpine@sha256:…` and `node:22-alpine@sha256:…`. · mech
- [ ] [UX·L·S] `docker/nginx-default.conf:20-23` — `/api/feedback` returns 404 `{"error":"not_available"}`, so the client toasts "not_available" (B5) and shows no GitHub fallback. → Return `503 {"code":"NOT_CONFIGURED"}`, which the client already maps to the fallback link. · mech
- [ ] [SEC·L·S] `docker/nginx-default.conf:1-8` — No `server_tokens off;`, so the nginx version shows in the `Server` header and error pages. · mech
- [ ] [CODE·L·S] `Dockerfile:15` — It copies `.output/public` wholesale, so nginx serves `/_headers`, `/_redirects` and `/200.html` as public files. → Remove them in the builder. · mech
- [ ] [PERF·L·S] `docker/nginx-default.conf:7-8,26-29` — `/_nuxt/` gets `expires 1y` but not `immutable` (`_headers:21` has it), and there's no `gzip_vary on`. · mech
- [ ] [TOOLING·L·S] `.dockerignore:1-13` — It doesn't exclude `.claude/` (8.7 MB of worktrees plus `launch.json`), `tests/` or `docs/screenshots`, so the build context is bloated. · mech
- [ ] [SEC·L·S] `public/_headers:9` — `form-action … https://github.com` is unused: every form uses `@submit.prevent`, and the GitHub fallback is a link. `worker-src blob:` is also unused (no Worker code). → Remove both. · mech
- [ ] [SEC·L·S] `public/_headers:9` — NOT FIXED (audit #919, "add report-to"). There's no `report-to`/`report-uri`, so a hash mismatch after a Nuxt upgrade (which blocks the importmap and `__NUXT__` scripts) would break prod boot without any signal. → Add `report-to` to a tiny Function or a reporting service. · judgment
- [ ] [CODE·L·S] `public/_redirects:1-2` — The file holds only comments that call the 404.html behaviour an "SPA fallback". → Delete it once E1 is fixed. · mech
- [ ] [CODE·L·S] `scripts/spa-fallback.mjs:3` — The header says it syncs `404.html / 200.html`, but it copies only 404. `200.html` comes from Nuxt and is still shipped (audit #921 asked for it to be removed). → Fix the comment and remove `200.html`. · mech

### Build, CI & tooling: package.json, ci.yml, vitest.config.ts, wrangler.toml
- [ ] [TOOLING·M·S] `package.json:19` — `lint` just prints "eslint not configured" and exits 0, so any human or bot running `npm run lint` sees green. → Remove the script, or add eslint (vue + a11y plugins). · decision
- [ ] [TOOLING·M·S] `package.json:23` — NOT FIXED (audit #927, "deploy from CI only"). `cf:deploy` still passes `--commit-dirty=true`, and no workflow uses `cf:deploy:ci`. → Add a main-branch deploy job using `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`, and drop the dirty flag. · decision
- [ ] [TOOLING·M·S] `.github/workflows/ci.yml:26` + `vitest.config.ts:8-18` — Coverage thresholds are configured but CI runs `vitest run` without `--coverage`, so they're never enforced. They are also only 15/10/8%, and `include` covers only `app/utils` and `functions` (no stores or composables). → Run `vitest run --coverage` in CI and raise the floor. · mech
- [ ] [TOOLING·M·S] `package.json:26-34` — `nuxt` and `@pinia/nuxt` are in `dependencies`, so `npm audit --omit=dev` reports 14 issues (6 critical: simple-git via @nuxt/devtools, node-forge via listhen) even though the static SPA ships none of them. CI has no audit step, so a real runtime advisory (dompurify, masto) would get lost. → Move build-only packages to devDependencies and add `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high` to CI. · judgment
- [ ] [TOOLING·L·S] `package.json:9-10` — `dev` and `build` don't run prepaint (only `generate` does), so editing appearance options shows a stale `prepaint.js` in dev. `build` makes a Node-server output this static deploy never uses, yet README.md:137 calls it "Production build". → Add `predev`/`prebuild` hooks, and relabel or remove `build`. · mech
- [ ] [TOOLING·L·S] `package.json:1-46` + `README.md:286` — README says "MIT", but there's no LICENSE file and no `"license"` field. · decision
- [ ] [TOOLING·L·S] `package.json:17-18` — `typecheck` checks only `functions/`, while `typecheck:app` checks the app, the opposite of what the names suggest. → Rename to `typecheck:functions`, with `typecheck` running both. · mech
- [ ] [TOOLING·L·S] `package.json:39-43` — vitest and coverage-v8 are on 2.1.9 (latest 5.x), vue-tsc is on 3.0.8 (3.3.12 is in range), and nuxt is on 4.5.2 (4.6.0 is in range). → Bump; the in-range updates are free. · mech
- [ ] [SEC·L·S] `.github/workflows/ci.yml:20-21` — Third-party actions are pinned by tag (`@v4`), not commit SHA, and the job has no `timeout-minutes`. · mech
- [ ] [TOOLING·L·S] `.github/workflows/ci.yml:23` + `wrangler.toml:6` — CI hardcodes `'22'` instead of using `node-version-file: .nvmrc`, and the Pages `compatibility_date` (2025-07-15) is 15 months old. · mech
- [ ] [TOOLING·L·M] `.github/workflows/ci.yml:16-29` — CI never builds the Docker image or runs `nginx -t`, so the second deploy target can quietly break. → Add a `docker build` job (on PR, or path-filtered). · judgment

### Tests: tests/*.test.ts
- [ ] [TOOLING·M·S] `tests/platform-security.test.ts:220-233` + `tests/notebookHandoff.test.ts:4-19` — The origin checks are tested only one by one; nothing tests `useLoomHandoff`'s dispatch, so C1 (loom.ibm.io sent to the notebook handler) passes the suite. → Test `onLoomMessage` routing with `{origin:'https://loom.ibm.io', data:{type:'loom-neospace-share',v:1}}`, plus the same for localhost and bruh/notebook. · judgment
  - *Verifier:* True: no test imports `useLoomHandoff` or exercises `onLoomMessage` dispatch, so #23 passes the suite. But a test gap isn't High impact on its own; the defect is #23. Impact: Medium.
- [ ] [TOOLING·M·S] `tests/smoke.test.ts:329-338` — "feedback area path" only checks `typeof createGitHubIssue === 'function'` and splits a string literal; no app code is exercised. → Test `createGitHubIssue` with stubbed `fetch` and `location` (sends the path only; a non-JSON reply throws `FeedbackError` `BAD_RESPONSE`). · mech
- [ ] [TOOLING·M·S] `tests/platform-security.test.ts:309-316` — `expect([400, 200]).toContain(res.status)` accepts either result for every body. If `null` or `[]` started returning 200 (an issue filed from junk), the test would still pass. → Give each case its own expected status. · mech
- [ ] [TOOLING·M·M] `functions/api/feedback.ts:202-237,287-300` — Branches with no tests: `FORBIDDEN_ORIGIN`, `RATE_LIMITED` + `Retry-After`, `TURNSTILE_REQUIRED`/`FAILED` (`verifyTurnstile`), the label-422 retry in `createIssue`, the `GITHUB_AUTH` mapping, and the `escapeMd` output (A3 would show up there). · mech
- [ ] [TOOLING·M·S] `tests/platform-security.test.ts:341-349` — "keeps valid screenshots" locks in the `data:` URI embed that GitHub won't render (A3), so the test protects the bug. → Rewrite it once A3 is decided. · judgment
- [ ] [TOOLING·M·M] `app/stores/composeHandoff.ts:213-238,318-383` — `ingestFromMessage`'s 25 MB and type caps, the 400 KB/500 KB saved-share caps, the image-name cleanup and the `ingestStored` de-dup/upgrade logic have no tests, though they guard against postMessage abuse (audit SEC items). · mech
- [ ] [TOOLING·L·S] `app/utils/feedback.ts:29-61,165-175` — No tests for `getGitHubNewIssueUrl` (truncation, URL length cap, when the clipboard is written) or for `prepareScreenshot`. · mech
- [ ] [TOOLING·L·M] `scripts/spa-fallback.mjs:39-63` + `scripts/build-prepaint.mjs:17-27` + `public/boot/rescue.js` — No tests for the CSP hash extraction or rewrite, the prepaint option extraction, or the rescue regex. A Nuxt upgrade that changes inline scripts could break boot under the enforced CSP with CI still green. → Add node tests against fixture HTML and `_headers`. · judgment
- [ ] [TOOLING·L·S] `tests/edwardDiscovery.test.ts:101-109` — "hides excluded moods" takes the mood from the ball under test and only checks that 'f' is gone. If every ball got the same mood, the list would be empty and the test would still pass. "ships channels…" (`:106-110`) only checks for a non-empty query and a truthy sort. → Assert the expected mood value and that 'h' and 't' remain; check that each channel's sort is a known value. · mech
- [ ] [TOOLING·L·S] `tests/smoke.test.ts:429-436` + `tests/inbox.test.ts:171` — Unmocked `console.error` prints "chunk load" and a full "Failed to dismiss notification" stack to stderr on every run, which buries real warnings. The log-ring test also doesn't call `clearLogRing()` first, so it depends on test order. · mech
- [ ] [TOOLING·L·S] `tests/federation.test.ts:64-71` — `vi.useRealTimers()` runs only if the assertions pass, so a failure leaves fake timers on for later tests. → `afterEach(() => vi.useRealTimers())`. · mech
